===== 4.23 `dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_error_series_no_incr_write_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_err_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: SEP OTP no-increment write error

*Test Coverage*:

* JTAG2AXI fixed-address (no-incr) write series through the SEP OTP AXI4-Lite bridge (IR=0x22)
* Mid-series SLVERR/DECERR injected on one beat; AXI-Lite address remains fixed for every beat
* Op-status identifies the failing beat while good beats commit correctly to the fixed address
* Bridge recovers for a subsequent legal access

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Configure the `ocah_axi_vip` AXI4-Lite subordinate to return a non-OKAY response on a selected beat at the fixed target address.
. Load SEP_OTP_AXI_SINGLE_OP (IR=0x22) and program a fixed-address write series of N beats.
. Run the series; the bridge holds the AXI-Lite address constant across beats on `axil_sep_otp_jtag_req_o`.
. Poll the op-status after each beat until not Busy.
. Verify the failing beat reports SLVERR/DECERR, good beats committed correctly to the fixed address, and the address never incremented.
. Issue a follow-up legal write; verify OKAY, proving recovery.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`sep_otp_axi_err_noincr_wr_status_chk` |Op-status flags the failing beat with the injected error code
|`sep_otp_axi_err_noincr_wr_mem_chk` |Good beats commit to the fixed address; no address increment observed
|`sep_otp_axi_err_noincr_wr_recover_chk` |A subsequent legal write completes with OKAY
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The injected fault is reported on the failing beat, the address stays fixed, good beats commit correctly, and the bridge recovers, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-424-dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_error_series_no_incr_read_test]]
===== 4.24 `dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_error_series_no_incr_read_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_err_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: SEP OTP no-increment read error

*Test Coverage*:

* JTAG2AXI fixed-address (no-incr) read series through the SEP OTP AXI4-Lite bridge (IR=0x22)
* Mid-series SLVERR/DECERR injected on one beat while the AXI-Lite address remains fixed
* Good beats return the reference value at the fixed address; failing beat marks invalid data
* Op-status error-information matches the injected fault; bridge recovers afterward

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Configure the `ocah_axi_vip` AXI4-Lite subordinate with a known value at the fixed address and a non-OKAY response on a selected read beat.
. Load SEP_OTP_AXI_SINGLE_OP (IR=0x22) and program a fixed-address read series of N beats.
. Run the series; the bridge holds the AXI-Lite read address constant across beats.
. Poll the op-status after each beat until not Busy.
. Verify good beats return the reference data, the failing beat reports SLVERR/DECERR with invalid data, and the address never incremented.
. Issue a follow-up legal read; verify OKAY and correct data, proving recovery.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`sep_otp_axi_err_noincr_rd_status_chk` |Op-status flags the failing read beat with the injected error code
|`sep_otp_axi_err_noincr_rd_rdata_chk` |Good beats match the fixed-address reference; failing beat data is invalid
|`sep_otp_axi_err_noincr_rd_recover_chk` |A subsequent legal read returns the reference data with OKAY
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Good beats match the fixed-address reference, the failing beat reports the injected error, the address stays fixed, and the bridge recovers, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-425-dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_error_series_incr_write_with_status_test]]
===== 4.25 `dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_error_series_incr_write_with_status_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_err_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: SEP OTP write with error-status TDR

*Test Coverage*:

* JTAG2AXI incrementing-address write series through the SEP OTP AXI4-Lite bridge (IR=0x22) with a fault mid-series
* Sticky error-status TDR scanned out after the series to retrieve the latched failing-beat fault
* Error-status TDR identifies the failing beat/address and SLVERR/DECERR code without per-beat polling
* Sticky status clears as defined; bridge recovers for a subsequent legal access

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Configure the `ocah_axi_vip` AXI4-Lite subordinate to return a non-OKAY response at one address within the incrementing range.
. Load SEP_OTP_AXI_SINGLE_OP (IR=0x22) and run an incrementing-address write series of N beats to completion.
. Scan out the sticky error-status TDR via Capture-DR/Shift-DR and decode the latched failing-beat index, address, and SLVERR/DECERR code.
. Verify good beats committed at their incremented addresses and the latched status matches the injected fault.
. Clear the sticky status, then issue a follow-up legal write; verify OKAY, proving recovery.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`sep_otp_axi_err_incr_wr_status_tdr_chk` |Error-status TDR latches the failing beat/address and SLVERR/DECERR code
|`sep_otp_axi_err_incr_wr_status_mem_chk` |Good beats commit at correctly-incremented addresses
|`sep_otp_axi_err_incr_wr_status_recover_chk` |Sticky status clears and a subsequent legal write completes with OKAY
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The sticky error-status TDR correctly reports the failing beat and error code, good beats commit, the status clears, and the bridge recovers, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-426-dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_error_series_incr_read_with_status_test]]
===== 4.26 `dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_error_series_incr_read_with_status_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_err_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: SEP OTP read with error-status TDR

*Test Coverage*:

* JTAG2AXI incrementing-address read series through the SEP OTP AXI4-Lite bridge (IR=0x22) with a fault mid-series
* Sticky error-status TDR scanned out after the series to retrieve the latched failing-beat fault
* Good beats before the fault return reference data; error-status TDR identifies the failing beat/address and SLVERR/DECERR code
* Sticky status clears as defined; bridge recovers for a subsequent legal access

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Pre-load the `ocah_axi_vip` AXI4-Lite subordinate with reference data and configure a non-OKAY response at one address in the incrementing range.
. Load SEP_OTP_AXI_SINGLE_OP (IR=0x22) and run an incrementing-address read series of N beats to completion.
. Scan out the sticky error-status TDR and decode the latched failing-beat index, address, and SLVERR/DECERR code.
. Verify good beats returned the reference data at their incremented addresses and the latched status matches the injected fault.
. Clear the sticky status, then issue a follow-up legal read; verify OKAY and correct data, proving recovery.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`sep_otp_axi_err_incr_rd_status_tdr_chk` |Error-status TDR latches the failing read beat/address and SLVERR/DECERR code
|`sep_otp_axi_err_incr_rd_status_rdata_chk` |Good beats match the incremented-address reference data
|`sep_otp_axi_err_incr_rd_status_recover_chk` |Sticky status clears and a subsequent legal read returns the reference data with OKAY
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The sticky error-status TDR correctly reports the failing read beat and error code, good beats match references, the status clears, and the bridge recovers, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-427-dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_error_security_gating_test]]
===== 4.27 `dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_error_security_gating_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_jtag2axi_sep_otp_axi_err_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: SEP OTP error-path security gating

*Test Coverage*:

* SEP OTP bridge gating: `sep_otp_jtag2axi_security_disable = !feat_ctrl_i.fuse_test || !feat_ctrl_i.sep_debug || !feat_ctrl_i.soc_debug || !feat_ctrl_i.ap_debug`
* When gated, SEP_OTP_AXI_SINGLE_OP (IR=0x22) falls back to BYPASS (1-bit DR) and issues no AXI-Lite traffic on `axil_sep_otp_jtag_req_o`, even on error-path attempts
* Each required enable low (`fuse_test`, `sep_debug`, `soc_debug`, `ap_debug`) disables the bridge; restoring it high restores operation
* Error-path read/write attempts while gated produce no subordinate access and no error-status update

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. With the AXI-Lite subordinate configured to return errors on the target region, drive required enable `feat_ctrl_i.ap_debug` = 0 to gate the bridge.
. Load SEP_OTP_AXI_SINGLE_OP (IR=0x22) and attempt an error-region read and write:
a. Verify the instruction selects BYPASS (1-bit DR).
b. Verify no AXI-Lite transaction is issued on `axil_sep_otp_jtag_req_o` and no error-status latches.
. Restore `feat_ctrl_i.ap_debug` = 1; verify normal/error-path operation resumes and the error is reported as expected.
. Repeat steps 1–3 for `feat_ctrl_i.soc_debug` = 0, `feat_ctrl_i.sep_debug` = 0, and `feat_ctrl_i.fuse_test` = 0, confirming each independently gates the bridge.
. Confirm that with all required enables high, ungated error-path accesses complete and report SLVERR/DECERR normally.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`sep_otp_axi_err_gating_bypass_chk` |Gated instruction selects BYPASS (1-bit DR) for each of `fuse_test`/`sep_debug`/`soc_debug`/`ap_debug`
|`sep_otp_axi_err_gating_no_traffic_chk` |No AXI-Lite traffic on `axil_sep_otp_jtag_req_o` while gated
|`sep_otp_axi_err_gating_restore_chk` |Restoring each required enable restores normal/error-path operation
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Any required enable low forces BYPASS with no AXI-Lite traffic on the error path, and operation is fully restored after each enable is restored, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-428-dtp_jtag2axi_backpressure_aw_before_w_test]]
===== 4.28 `dtp_jtag2axi_backpressure_aw_before_w_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_jtag2axi_backpressure_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: AW before W channel skew

*Test Coverage*:

* Maximum AW/W channel skew on the JTAG2AXI single-op write path with AW ready asserted while W ready is held low
* Reachable `AXI_SEND_ADDR_W -++>++ AXI_WAIT_BRESP` FSM transition on `axi_state_q_tclk`
* AXI-Lite `aw_valid_stable` / `w_valid_stable` and payload-stability assertions while ready is low
* Intended to support a documented waiver for `AXI_SEND_DATA_W` should that state remain structurally unreachable (AW{plus}W are pushed atomically through the per-channel `axi_cdc_clearable` FIFOs, so the FSM history is expected to skip it)

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Drive a JTAG2AXI single-op write through `OcahJtagTap`:
a. Program the bridge address and data via the primary TAP and launch the write op.
b. Configure the `ocah_axi_vip` subordinate to skew the channels: accept AW (ready high) while holding W ready low for an extended interval.
. Observe the bridge FSM and channels under skew:
a. Sample the FSM history mirror and confirm the sequence `AXI_IDLE -++>++ AXI_SEND_ADDR_W -++>++ AXI_WAIT_BRESP -++>++ AXI_UPDATE_STATUS -++>++ AXI_IDLE`.
b. Confirm `axi_state_q_tclk == AXI_SEND_DATA_W` is never observed at any sample point.
c. While W ready is low, confirm AW/W valid and payload remain stable and do not deassert.
. Release the W-channel backpressure and let the write drain.
. Verify the write completes with an OKAY response and read back the written location to confirm the value landed.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`j2a_bp_fsm_chk` |FSM traverses `AXI_SEND_ADDR_W -++>++ AXI_WAIT_BRESP` and never enters `AXI_SEND_DATA_W`
|`j2a_bp_stable_chk` |AW/W valid and payload remain stable while the subordinate holds ready low
|`j2a_bp_status_chk` |Final JTAG2AXI completion status is OKAY and read-back data matches the written value
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The reachable maximum-skew path and AXI-Lite stable assertions are exercised, the observed FSM history supports a waiver for the (expected structurally-unreachable) `AXI_SEND_DATA_W` state, and the write completes correctly with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-429-dtp_jtag2axi_backpressure_long_stall_test]]
===== 4.29 `dtp_jtag2axi_backpressure_long_stall_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_jtag2axi_backpressure_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: Long ready stalls

*Test Coverage*:

* Long subordinate ready stalls on AW, W, AR, and R channels exercised one channel at a time and combined
* Real success hits on the AXI-Lite `aw_valid_stable` / `w_valid_stable` / `ar_valid_stable` and corresponding payload `+*_stable+` assertions
* Reachable JTAG2AXI FSM dwell in `AXI_SEND_ADDR_W` / `AXI_WAIT_BRESP` (write) and the read-issue / wait states under prolonged stall
* No premature timeout, abort, or status corruption while a legal stall is in progress

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Set up stall stimulus on the `ocah_axi_vip` subordinate:
a. Configure long, deterministic ready-low intervals on AW/W (write path) and AR/R (read path) that remain inside the architectural stall budget.
. Issue write traffic through `OcahJtagTap`:
a. Launch a single-op write while AW/W ready are stalled; observe valid/payload hold stable across the full stall window.
b. Release the stall and confirm OKAY completion.
. Issue read traffic through `OcahJtagTap`:
a. Launch a single-op read while AR ready (then R ready) is stalled; observe AR valid/address hold stable.
b. Release the stall and confirm the returned data matches the reference model.
. Verify the bridge does not declare a timeout or error during any legal stall and that each operation reaches its expected terminal status after release.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`j2a_long_stall_assert_chk` |AW/W/AR valid and payload `+*_stable+` assertions hold across the full stall window
|`j2a_long_stall_status_chk` |No premature timeout/abort; each op reaches its expected terminal status after stall release
|`j2a_long_stall_data_chk` |Post-stall write-back and read-data compare against the reference model
|===

*Pass Criteria*: All AW/W/AR stable assertions receive real success hits under long stalls, no operation times out or aborts during a legal stall, and every read/write completes correctly after release with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-430-dtp_jtag2axi_backpressure_abort_at_data_w_test]]
===== 4.30 `dtp_jtag2axi_backpressure_abort_at_data_w_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_jtag2axi_backpressure_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: Reset abort around data phase

*Test Coverage*:

* Reset/abort asserted from reachable JTAG2AXI FSM states while a write is stalled at the data phase
* In-flight bridge state and per-channel `axi_cdc_clearable` FIFO contents are flushed on abort
* No partial or stale write escapes to the subordinate after the abort
* Clean recovery: a subsequent legal write/read completes normally from `AXI_IDLE`

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Stage a stalled write through `OcahJtagTap`:
a. Launch a single-op write and hold the subordinate W ready low so the bridge dwells in the reachable write FSM states (`AXI_SEND_ADDR_W` / `AXI_WAIT_BRESP`).
. Assert reset/abort mid-flight:
a. Drive the JTAG2AXI reset/abort while the operation is held, exercising the abort transition out of the reachable FSM state.
b. Sample the FSM history and confirm the state returns to `AXI_IDLE` and never lands in `AXI_SEND_DATA_W`.
. Confirm containment:
a. Verify the `ocah_axi_vip` subordinate observed no completed write (no escaped partial transaction) for the aborted op.
b. Verify the bridge status reports the abort/idle condition rather than a spurious OKAY.
. Issue a fresh clean write and read-back to confirm full recovery.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`j2a_abort_fsm_chk` |Abort drives the FSM back to `AXI_IDLE` from a reachable state without entering `AXI_SEND_DATA_W`
|`j2a_abort_escape_chk` |No partial/stale write reaches the subordinate for the aborted operation
|`j2a_abort_recovery_chk` |A subsequent legal write/read completes correctly after the abort
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The reset/abort transition fires from a reachable FSM state, in-flight state is fully flushed with no escaped write, and the bridge recovers to service a clean follow-on access with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-431-dtp_jtag2axi_cdc_clear_abort_narrow_reset_mid_xaction_test]]
===== 4.31 `dtp_jtag2axi_cdc_clear_abort_narrow_reset_mid_xaction_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_jtag2axi_cdc_clear_abort_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: Narrow reset during transaction

*Test Coverage*:

* `cdc_reset_ctrlr_half` reset-driven intermediate recovery states reached by a narrow reset pulse mid-transaction
* TCK-to-ACLK CDC FIFO clear / isolate handshake while bridge traffic is in flight
* Bridge status converges to a defined abort/idle condition after the narrow pulse
* Subsequent access completes normally, proving the CDC recovers cleanly

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Launch a transaction across the CDC through `OcahJtagTap`:
a. Start a JTAG2AXI op so traffic is mid-flight across the TCK-to-ACLK `axi_cdc_clearable` path.
. Apply a narrow reset pulse:
a. Pulse the CDC clear/reset for a short interval timed to land mid-transaction, exercising the `cdc_reset_ctrlr_half` intermediate clear/isolate recovery states.
b. Sample the CDC controller state and confirm it passes through the expected intermediate recovery states rather than hanging.
. Verify post-pulse status reports abort/idle and the CDC FIFOs are emptied/cleared.
. Issue a follow-on read/write and confirm it completes with the expected status and data.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`j2a_cdc_narrow_state_chk` |`cdc_reset_ctrlr_half` passes through the expected intermediate clear/isolate recovery states
|`j2a_cdc_narrow_status_chk` |Post-pulse bridge status converges to a defined abort/idle condition
|`j2a_cdc_narrow_recovery_chk` |A subsequent access completes correctly after the narrow reset
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The narrow reset pulse drives `cdc_reset_ctrlr_half` through its reachable intermediate recovery states, the CDC FIFOs clear, status reaches a defined abort/idle condition, and a follow-on access succeeds with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-432-dtp_jtag2axi_cdc_clear_abort_back_to_back_reset_test]]
===== 4.32 `dtp_jtag2axi_cdc_clear_abort_back_to_back_reset_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_jtag2axi_cdc_clear_abort_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: Back-to-back CDC reset recovery

*Test Coverage*:

* CDC reset re-entry with back-to-back reset pulses across the TCK-to-ACLK domains
* `cdc_reset_ctrlr_half` IDLE / ISOLATE / CLEAR recovery-path coverage including re-entry while a prior clear is still settling
* All bridge FIFOs and FSMs converge to a clean idle after repeated resets (no deadlock or stuck handshake)
* Post-recovery reads and writes complete correctly across all involved clock domains

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Start active bridge traffic through `OcahJtagTap`.
. Apply back-to-back CDC resets:
a. Issue a second reset pulse before the first clear/isolate sequence has fully settled, forcing CDC reset re-entry.
b. Sample the CDC controller and confirm it cycles through the IDLE / ISOLATE / CLEAR recovery states and does not deadlock on re-entry.
. Verify all bridge FIFOs and FSMs settle to a clean idle after the reset burst.
. Issue subsequent read and write operations and confirm each reaches its expected terminal status with correct data.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`j2a_cdc_b2b_state_chk` |CDC controller exercises IDLE/ISOLATE/CLEAR recovery paths through reset re-entry without deadlock
|`j2a_cdc_b2b_settle_chk` |All bridge FIFOs/FSMs return to a clean idle after the back-to-back reset burst
|`j2a_cdc_b2b_recovery_chk` |Post-recovery reads/writes complete correctly with expected status and data
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Back-to-back CDC resets exercise the IDLE/ISOLATE/CLEAR recovery paths and reset re-entry without deadlock, all bridge state settles cleanly, and subsequent operations complete correctly with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-433-dtp_jtag2axi_decode_error_decerr_write_test]]
===== 4.33 `dtp_jtag2axi_decode_error_decerr_write_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_jtag2axi_decode_err_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local XTRIG monitor

*Objective*: CTN default-slave write DECERR

*Test Coverage*:

* CTN AXI-Lite xbar default-slave write DECERR path triggered by a write to an unmapped address
* Xbar `+default_*_mst_port[_en]+` write-decode branch and default-slave response generation
* No mapped CTN register receives a side effect from the unmapped write
* Bridge recovers and accepts a subsequent legal mapped write

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Issue an unmapped write:
a. Drive a JTAG2AXI write to an address with no valid CTN xbar slave so it routes to the default DECERR slave.
. Verify the error response and monitor:
a. Confirm the write completion reports DECERR.
b. Confirm the OCAH-local XTRIG monitor observes the decode miss / default-slave routing event.
. Verify no side effects:
a. Compare all mapped CTN register/memory contents against the reference model and confirm none changed.
. Issue a follow-on legal mapped write and confirm it completes with OKAY and the expected value lands.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`j2a_decerr_wr_status_chk` |Unmapped write reports DECERR via the default-slave path
|`j2a_decerr_wr_mem_chk` |No mapped CTN register/memory side effect from the unmapped write
|`j2a_decerr_wr_recovery_chk` |A subsequent legal mapped write completes with OKAY
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The unmapped write routes to the CTN xbar default DECERR slave and returns DECERR, no mapped register is disturbed, the XTRIG monitor witnesses the decode miss, and a follow-on legal write succeeds with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-434-dtp_jtag2axi_decode_error_decerr_read_test]]
===== 4.34 `dtp_jtag2axi_decode_error_decerr_read_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_jtag2axi_decode_err_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local XTRIG monitor

*Objective*: CTN default-slave read DECERR

*Test Coverage*:

* CTN AXI-Lite xbar default-slave read DECERR path triggered by a read from an unmapped address
* Xbar `+default_ar_mst_port[_en]+` read-decode branch and default-slave defined read-data response
* No mapped CTN register receives a read-induced side effect (e.g., clear-on-read fields untouched)
* Bridge recovers and accepts a subsequent legal mapped read

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Issue an unmapped read:
a. Drive a JTAG2AXI read from an address with no valid CTN xbar slave so it routes to the default DECERR slave.
. Verify the error response and read data:
a. Confirm the read completion reports DECERR with the defined default read-data value.
b. Confirm the OCAH-local XTRIG monitor observes the read decode miss / default-slave routing event.
. Verify no mapped read-side side effects against the reference model.
. Issue a follow-on legal mapped read and confirm DECERR is not reported and the returned data matches the reference model.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`j2a_decerr_rd_status_chk` |Unmapped read reports DECERR via the default-slave path
|`j2a_decerr_rd_rdata_chk` |Returned read data matches the defined default-slave value
|`j2a_decerr_rd_recovery_chk` |A subsequent legal mapped read returns correct data without DECERR
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The unmapped read routes to the CTN xbar default DECERR slave and returns DECERR with defined read data, no mapped register side effect occurs, the XTRIG monitor witnesses the decode miss, and a follow-on legal read succeeds with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-435-dtp_jtag2axi_decode_error_mixed_test]]
===== 4.35 `dtp_jtag2axi_decode_error_mixed_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_jtag2axi_decode_err_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local XTRIG monitor

*Objective*: Mixed mapped/unmapped accesses

*Test Coverage*:

* Alternating mapped and unmapped CTN xbar accesses for stable-path and default-path coverage in the same run
* Both mapped (OKAY) and default-slave (DECERR) routing decisions exercised back to back
* Ordering and per-access status preserved across the mapped/unmapped interleave
* No cross-contamination: an unmapped access neither disturbs mapped state nor corrupts the status of an adjacent mapped access

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Build an interleaved access stream through the JTAG2AXI path:
a. Alternate mapped accesses (valid CTN slave) with unmapped accesses (default DECERR slave), covering both reads and writes.
. Drive the stream and capture per-access outcomes:
a. Confirm each mapped access completes with OKAY and the expected data/side effect.
b. Confirm each unmapped access completes with DECERR via the default slave.
c. Confirm the OCAH-local XTRIG monitor logs each decode decision in issue order.
. Verify ordering and isolation:
a. Confirm status and ordering are preserved across the interleave and that mapped register state is unaffected by the interleaved unmapped accesses.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`j2a_mixed_status_chk` |Each mapped access reports OKAY and each unmapped access reports DECERR
|`j2a_mixed_order_chk` |Per-access status and ordering preserved across the mapped/unmapped interleave
|`j2a_mixed_isolation_chk` |Mapped register state and data unaffected by interleaved unmapped accesses
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The mapped/unmapped interleave exercises both the stable mapped path and the default DECERR path with correct per-access status, ordering is preserved, mapped state is uncontaminated, and the XTRIG monitor witnesses every decode decision with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-436-dtp_jtag2axi_series_corner_all_bridges_test]]
===== 4.36 `dtp_jtag2axi_series_corner_all_bridges_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_jtag2axi_series_corner_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: Series corner cases across all bridges

*Test Coverage*:

* Series-operation FIFO depth corners (minimum, maximum, and full/near-full) across all three JTAG2AXI bridges
* Sticky status accumulation and clear behavior over multi-beat series operations
* Address bookkeeping for fixed and incrementing series, including aligned and unaligned start addresses
* Per-bridge independence: each enabled bridge tracks its own series state without aliasing the others
* Read pipelining via `SERIES_CTRL.pl_depth`: with `pl_depth=N`, up to `N{plus}1` reads may be outstanding; the first `N{plus}1` reads return the `DEADBEEF` marker and subsequent reads return real data in deterministic queued order; `pl_depth` swept up to each bridge's reported `+(rd/wr)_pl_depth+` cap

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. For each of the three enabled JTAG2AXI bridges, drive a sweep of series operations through `OcahJtagTap`:
a. Minimum-length and maximum-length series to exercise the series FIFO depth corners.
b. Aligned and unaligned start addresses to exercise address bookkeeping.
c. Fixed and incrementing series to exercise both address-progression modes.
d. Read series with `SERIES_CTRL.pl_depth` swept from 0 to the bridge's reported pipeline-depth cap, issuing `pl_depth{plus}1` outstanding reads.
. Capture per-operation results from the `ocah_axi_vip` subordinate:
a. Confirm the series FIFO accepts each beat up to its depth and reports the expected status at the FIFO corners.
b. Confirm sticky status accumulates correctly across the series and clears as defined.
c. Confirm the final address matches the expected fixed/incrementing progression for each series length.
d. For the pipelined reads, confirm the first `pl_depth{plus}1` reads return the `DEADBEEF` marker and subsequent reads return the subordinate data in deterministic queued order.
. Verify per-bridge isolation: repeat across all three bridges and confirm no bridge's series state, status, or address bookkeeping aliases another.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`j2a_series_fifo_chk` |Series FIFO behaves correctly at minimum/maximum/full depth corners on each bridge
|`j2a_series_status_chk` |Sticky status accumulates and clears correctly across each series operation
|`j2a_series_addr_chk` |Final address matches the expected fixed/incrementing progression for aligned and unaligned starts
|`j2a_series_pldepth_chk` |With `pl_depth=N`, the first `N{plus}1` reads return `DEADBEEF` and later reads return real data in queued order
|===

*Pass Criteria*: All series corner cases (FIFO depth, sticky status, address bookkeeping, and `pl_depth` read pipelining with DEADBEEF marker behavior) complete as expected on each of the three bridges, with no cross-bridge aliasing and zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-5-stap-3dcr-and-ijtag-scenarios]]
==== 5. STAP, 3DCR, and iJTAG Scenarios

[[sec-51-dtp_ijtag_sib_all_off_test]]
===== 5.1 `dtp_ijtag_sib_all_off_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_ijtag_sib_all_off_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, OCAH-local iJTAG SIB model

*Objective*: All SIBs OFF

*Test Coverage*:

* SELECT_IJTAG (IR=0x1A) with every SIB control bit driven to 0 yields the minimum-length scan chain
* DR chain length equals the SIB-control bits only (3 bits for sec_dft, dft, dfd) with no instrument registers inserted
* None of `jtag_dfd_host_scan_ctrl_o`, `jtag_dft_host_scan_ctrl_o`, `jtag_dft_secure_host_scan_ctrl_o` assert during Shift-DR
* Closed SIBs bypass their downstream instruments so no instrument capture/update occurs

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Load SELECT_IJTAG (IR=0x1A) into the PTAP via `OcahJtagTap`.
. Shift a SIB pattern with all three SIB bits = 0 and pulse Update-DR.
. Measure the active DR chain length.
a. Shift a known marker pattern and count the bit delay through the chain.
b. Verify the length equals the 3 SIB-control bits only (no instrument register widths added).
. During Shift-DR, sample all three scan-control outputs and verify none asserts (instruments are bypassed).
. Compare captured/update data against the OCAH-local iJTAG SIB model for the all-off configuration.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`sib_all_off_len_chk` |DR chain length equals SIB-control bits only with all instruments bypassed
|`sib_all_off_ctrl_chk` |No DFD/DFT/secure-DFT scan-control output asserts during Shift-DR
|`sib_all_off_data_chk` |Captured/update data matches the SIB reference model for the all-off path
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Chain length collapses to the SIB-control bits, all instrument scan controls stay inactive, and observed data matches the SIB reference model, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-52-dtp_ijtag_sib_all_on_test]]
===== 5.2 `dtp_ijtag_sib_all_on_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_ijtag_sib_all_on_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, OCAH-local iJTAG SIB model

*Objective*: All SIBs ON

*Test Coverage*:

* SELECT_IJTAG (IR=0x1A) with all SIB bits = 1 produces the full-length iJTAG scan chain
* DR chain length equals sum of SIB bits plus all three instrument register widths (3 SIBs {plus} 3 instruments)
* All three scan controls (`jtag_dfd_host_scan_ctrl_o`, `jtag_dft_host_scan_ctrl_o`, `jtag_dft_secure_host_scan_ctrl_o`) assert during Shift-DR
* `feat_ctrl_i.ap_debug=0` forces all three SIBs closed even when the scan pattern requests all-ON, collapsing the chain
* Access is restored when `ap_debug` returns to `1`

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Load SELECT_IJTAG (IR=0x1A) via `OcahJtagTap`.
. Shift a pattern enabling all SIBs (all bits = 1); Update-DR.
. Measure DR chain length by shifting a known marker and counting the delay.
a. Verify length equals the sum of SIB bits plus all three instrument register widths.
. Verify all three host scan-control outputs assert during Shift-DR and instrument data is captured correctly per the SIB model.
. Security gating:
a. Drive required enable `feat_ctrl_i.ap_debug=0` (disables DFD, secure DFT, non-secure DFT).
b. Re-shift the all-ON pattern; Update-DR.
c. Verify chain length collapses to SIB bits only and no instrument scan control asserts.
. Clear `ap_debug=0`; re-shift all-ON and confirm full chain and all scan controls restored.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`sib_all_on_len_chk` |Full chain length matches sum of SIB bits plus all three instrument widths
|`sib_all_on_ctrl_chk` |All three host scan-control outputs assert during Shift-DR
|`sib_all_on_gate_chk` |`ap_debug`=1 forces every SIB closed; chain collapses despite all-ON request
|`sib_all_on_restore_chk` |Full chain and scan controls restored after `ap_debug` clears
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The all-ON chain reaches full length with every scan control active, collapses to SIB bits when `ap_debug=0`, and recovers when restored, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-53-dtp_ijtag_sib_random_test]]
===== 5.3 `dtp_ijtag_sib_random_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_ijtag_sib_random_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, OCAH-local iJTAG SIB model

*Objective*: Exhaustive/random SIB patterns

*Test Coverage*:

* All 8 SIB on/off patterns (sec_dft x dft x dfd) walked exhaustively to close `ijtag_sib_pattern` coverage
* Predicted chain length matches sum of (SIB bit {plus} instrument width when SIB ON and ungated, else SIB bit only)
* Only enabled, ungated instruments receive their host scan-control assertions
* Randomized `feat_ctrl_i` (`soc_debug`, `ap_debug`, `sep_debug`, `fuse_test`) forces gated SIBs closed regardless of scan pattern
* Capture/update data tracks the SIB reference model across mixed configurations

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Load SELECT_IJTAG (IR=0x1A) via `OcahJtagTap`.
. Walk all 8 SIB patterns exhaustively:
a. Shift each (sec_dft, dft, dfd) pattern; Update-DR.
b. Measure chain length and verify it equals SIB bits plus the widths of enabled instruments.
c. Verify scan-control outputs assert only for the enabled instruments.
. For N additional randomized iterations (e.g., 20):
a. Drive random `feat_ctrl_i` fields and a random SIB pattern; Update-DR.
b. Verify gated SIBs (those with `security_disable_i` asserted) stay closed despite the requested pattern.
c. Verify chain length accounts for forced-closed SIBs and only non-gated enabled instruments receive scan control.
. Compare all captured/update data against the OCAH-local SIB model for each configuration.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`sib_random_len_chk` |Chain length matches predicted SIB-bit {plus} ungated-instrument-width sum
|`sib_random_ctrl_chk` |Scan control asserts only for enabled, ungated instruments
|`sib_random_gate_chk` |Security-gated SIBs forced closed under random `feat_ctrl_i`
|`sib_random_data_chk` |Captured/update data matches SIB reference model across all patterns
|===

*Pass Criteria*: All 8 SIB patterns plus randomized security combinations yield correct chain lengths, scan-control assertions, and gating behavior matching the reference model, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-54-dtp_ijtag_dft_test]]
===== 5.4 `dtp_ijtag_dft_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_ijtag_dft_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, OCAH-local iJTAG SIB model

*Objective*: DFT secure/non-secure access

*Test Coverage*:

* Independent access to the secure and non-secure DFT instruments via their SIBs (SELECT_IJTAG, IR=0x1A)
* Both DFT SIBs may be open simultaneously (no hardware mutual exclusion); chain length sums both instruments
* `dft_nonsecure_security_disable = !soc_debug || !ap_debug` gates the non-secure DFT SIB
* `dft_secure_security_disable = !fuse_test || !sep_debug || !soc_debug || !ap_debug` gates the secure DFT SIB
* Driving `soc_debug` low gates both SIBs; driving `sep_debug` or `fuse_test` low gates only the secure DFT SIB

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Load SELECT_IJTAG (IR=0x1A); enable the non-secure DFT SIB; Update-DR.
a. Verify `jtag_dft_host_scan_ctrl_o` asserts and shift a test pattern through the non-secure chain.
. Disable non-secure, enable secure DFT SIB; Update-DR.
a. Verify `jtag_dft_secure_host_scan_ctrl_o` asserts and shift a test pattern through the secure chain.
. Enable both DFT SIBs; Update-DR; verify both scan controls assert and chain length equals the sum of both instruments.
. Non-secure gating: drive `feat_ctrl_i.soc_debug=0`; verify both SIBs forced closed (soc_debug appears in both formulas); restore.
. Secure-only gating via `sep_debug=0`: verify secure DFT SIB forced closed while non-secure remains openable; restore.
. Secure-only gating via `fuse_test=0`: verify secure DFT SIB forced closed while non-secure remains openable; restore and confirm both SIBs restored.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`dft_access_chk` |Secure and non-secure DFT chains each accessible via their SIB with correct scan controls
|`dft_parallel_chk` |Both DFT SIBs open simultaneously; chain length sums both instruments
|`dft_nonsecure_gate_chk` |Non-secure DFT SIB forced closed under `soc_debug {vbar}{vbar} ap_debug`
|`dft_secure_gate_chk` |Secure DFT SIB forced closed when any required enable is low
|`dft_gate_restore_chk` |Both DFT SIBs restored after required `feat_ctrl_i` enables are high
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Secure and non-secure DFT instruments are independently reachable in the same scan flow, each gated by its own security formula and restored when required enables are high, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-55-dtp_ijtag_dfd_test]]
===== 5.5 `dtp_ijtag_dfd_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_ijtag_dfd_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, OCAH-local iJTAG SIB model

*Objective*: DFD access

*Test Coverage*:

* DFD instrument reachable through the iJTAG network when its SIB is open (SELECT_IJTAG, IR=0x1A)
* `jtag_dfd_host_scan_ctrl_o` asserts shift control and data shifts correctly through `jtag_dfd_host_scan_out_o` / `jtag_dfd_host_scan_in_i`
* `feat_ctrl_i.ap_debug=0` forces the DFD SIB closed (gated), restored when `ap_debug=1`

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Load SELECT_IJTAG (IR=0x1A) via `OcahJtagTap`; open only the DFD SIB; Update-DR.
. Verify `jtag_dfd_host_scan_ctrl_o` asserts; shift a known pattern out on `jtag_dfd_host_scan_out_o` and read it back through `jtag_dfd_host_scan_in_i` via the OCAH-local SIB model.
. Drive `feat_ctrl_i.ap_debug=0`; attempt to open the DFD SIB.
a. Verify the SIB stays closed (`security_disable_i` prevents it latching ON) and `jtag_dfd_host_scan_ctrl_o` does not assert.
. Restore `ap_debug=1`; re-open the DFD SIB and verify DFD access is restored.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`dfd_access_chk` |DFD scan data shifts correctly when the SIB is open and ungated
|`dfd_gate_chk` |DFD SIB forced closed and scan-ctrl inactive while `ap_debug`=1
|`dfd_restore_chk` |DFD access resumes after `ap_debug` clears
|===

*Pass Criteria*: DFD is reachable when ungated and forced closed when `ap_debug=0`, recovering when restored, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-56-dtp_3dcr_stap_sel_ds_test]]
===== 5.6 `dtp_3dcr_stap_sel_ds_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_3dcr_stap_sel_ds_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, OCAH-local STAP model

*Objective*: Downstream STAP selection

*Test Coverage*:

* TAP_3DCR (IR=0x0E) selects the downstream (I/O) STAP by writing 3DCR `stap_sel=1`, `tms_hold=1`, `config_hold=1`
* Configuration opens the DS SIB, writes the 3DCR, and closes the SIB in one DR scan so subsequent scans carry only a 1-bit SIB per STAP
* Six data patterns (all-zeros, all-ones, alt-10, alt-01, walking-ones, walking-zeros) transfer correctly to the DS PTAP slave model
* `feat_ctrl_i.sip_debug=0` gates the DS STAP; while gated the DS PTAP model is not updated
* Recovery requires restoring `sip_debug=1`, JTAG reset (TRST) to clear the stale `stap_sel`, and reconfiguration

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Load TAP_3DCR (IR=0x0E) into the PTAP via `OcahJtagTap`.
. Configure the DS STAP: open the DS SIB, write DS 3DCR `stap_sel=1, tms_hold=1, config_hold=1`, then close the DS SIB in the same DR scan; SEP and extra STAPs stay bypassed.
. Scan the extended IR: PTAP loaded with BYPASS, each STAP's SIB bit kept closed, DS downstream TAP IR included for the selected STAP.
. For each of the 6 data patterns, scan the extended DR (PTAP BYPASS 1 bit {plus} STAP SIB bits {plus} DS downstream TAP DR), read the DS PTAP model, and verify the readback matches.
. Security gating:
a. Drive `feat_ctrl_i.sip_debug=0`, reconfigure the DS STAP, and verify the DS PTAP model is unchanged (gated).
b. Restore required enables, assert JTAG reset (TRST) to clear the stale gated `stap_sel=1` condition.
c. Reconfigure the DS STAP, re-scan extended IR/DR with new data, and verify the DS PTAP model updates (access restored).

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`stap_ds_select_chk` |DS STAP TAP control active and DS SIB closed after 3DCR configuration
|`stap_ds_data_chk` |All 6 data patterns match the DS PTAP slave model readback
|`stap_ds_gate_chk` |DS PTAP model unchanged while `sip_debug`=0
|`stap_ds_recover_chk` |DS access restored after restoring `sip_debug` {plus} TRST {plus} reconfiguration
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The downstream STAP is selected and reachable with the SIB closed for minimal chain length, all six patterns verify, and gating plus recovery behave per `sip_debug`, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-57-dtp_3dcr_stap_sel_smc_test]]
===== 5.7 `dtp_3dcr_stap_sel_smc_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_3dcr_stap_sel_smc_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, OCAH-local STAP model

*Objective*: SMC STAP selection

*Test Coverage*:

* TAP_3DCR (IR=0x0E) selects the SMC debug STAP via 3DCR `stap_sel=1`, `tms_hold=1`, `config_hold=1`
* Configuration opens the SMC SIB, writes the 3DCR, and closes the SIB in one DR scan to minimize subsequent chain length
* Six data patterns transfer correctly to the SMC PTAP slave model
* SMC STAP gated by `!soc_debug || !ap_debug`; while gated the SMC PTAP model is not updated
* Recovery requires restoring required enables, JTAG reset (TRST), and reconfiguration

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Load TAP_3DCR (IR=0x0E) via `OcahJtagTap`.
. Configure the SMC STAP: open the SMC SIB, write SMC 3DCR `stap_sel=1, tms_hold=1, config_hold=1`, then close the SMC SIB in the same DR scan; DS, SEP, and extra STAPs stay bypassed.
. Scan the extended IR: PTAP BYPASS, STAP SIB bits kept closed, SMC downstream TAP IR included.
. For each of the 6 data patterns, scan the extended DR through PTAP BYPASS {plus} STAP SIB bits {plus} SMC downstream TAP DR; read and verify the SMC PTAP model.
. Security gating:
a. Drive `feat_ctrl_i.soc_debug=0`, reconfigure, and verify the SMC PTAP model is unchanged (gated by `!soc_debug || !ap_debug`).
b. Restore required enables, assert JTAG reset (TRST) to clear the stale `stap_sel`.
c. Reconfigure, re-scan extended IR/DR, and verify the SMC PTAP model updates (access restored).

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`stap_smc_select_chk` |SMC STAP TAP control active and SMC SIB closed after configuration
|`stap_smc_data_chk` |All 6 data patterns match the SMC PTAP slave model readback
|`stap_smc_gate_chk` |SMC PTAP model unchanged while `soc_debug`=1
|`stap_smc_recover_chk` |SMC access restored after restoring enables {plus} TRST {plus} reconfiguration
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The SMC STAP is selected with the SIB closed, all six patterns verify, and `!soc_debug || !ap_debug` gating plus TRST-based recovery behave correctly, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-58-dtp_3dcr_stap_sel_extra_test]]
===== 5.8 `dtp_3dcr_stap_sel_extra_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_3dcr_stap_sel_extra_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, OCAH-local STAP model

*Objective*: Extra STAP selection

*Test Coverage*:

* TAP_3DCR (IR=0x0E) independently selects each extra STAP (e.g. Yayoi TAP) via 3DCR `stap_sel=1`, `tms_hold=1`, `config_hold=1`
* Configuration opens the extra STAP SIB, writes the 3DCR, and closes the SIB so the 3DCR is removed and only a 1-bit SIB remains in subsequent scans
* `jtag_stap_extra_host_tap_ctrl_o[0]` forwards TAP control and the extra STAP TDO inserts into the scan chain
* `stap_extra_security_disable = !ap_debug` gates the extra STAPs; while gated the extra STAP model is not updated
* Recovery requires restoring `ap_debug=1`, JTAG reset (TRST), and reconfiguration

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Load TAP_3DCR (IR=0x0E) via `OcahJtagTap`.
. Configure extra STAP[0]: open its SIB, write extra STAP 3DCR `stap_sel=1, tms_hold=1, config_hold=1`, then close the SIB in the same DR scan.
. Scan the extended IR (PTAP BYPASS, all STAP SIB bits kept closed) and verify `jtag_stap_extra_host_tap_ctrl_o[0]` forwards TAP control.
. Scan the extended DR (PTAP BYPASS 1 bit {plus} STAP SIB bits {plus} downstream TAP DR) and verify the extra STAP[0] TDO is inserted; repeat for any additional configured extra STAPs.
. Security gating:
a. Drive `feat_ctrl_i.ap_debug=0`, reconfigure extra STAP[0], and verify the extra STAP model is unchanged (gated).
b. Restore required enables, assert JTAG reset (TRST) to clear stale 3DCR state.
c. Reconfigure extra STAP[0], re-scan extended IR/DR with new data, and verify the extra STAP model updates (access restored).

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`stap_extra_select_chk` |Each extra STAP independently selectable; SIB closed and 3DCR removed after configuration
|`stap_extra_ctrl_chk` |`jtag_stap_extra_host_tap_ctrl_o[0]` forwards TAP control and TDO inserts into the chain
|`stap_extra_gate_chk` |Extra STAP model unchanged while `ap_debug`=0 (`stap_extra_security_disable = !ap_debug`)
|`stap_extra_recover_chk` |Extra STAP access restored after restoring `ap_debug` {plus} TRST {plus} reconfiguration
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Each extra STAP is independently selectable with its SIB closed, TAP control and TDO route correctly, and `ap_debug` gating plus TRST-based recovery behave correctly, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-59-dtp_3dcr_stap_sel_sep_test]]
===== 5.9 `dtp_3dcr_stap_sel_sep_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_3dcr_stap_sel_sep_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, OCAH-local STAP model

*Objective*: SEP STAP selection

*Test Coverage*:

* TAP_3DCR (IR=0x0E) selects the SEP debug STAP via 3DCR `stap_sel=1`, `tms_hold=1`, `config_hold=1`
* Configuration opens the SEP SIB, writes the 3DCR, and closes the SIB in one DR scan to minimize subsequent chain length
* Extended IR carries the SEP PTAP instruction (opcode 0x3A) and six data patterns transfer correctly to the SEP PTAP slave model
* SEP STAP gated by `!sep_debug || !soc_debug || !ap_debug`; while gated the SEP PTAP model is not updated
* Recovery requires restoring required enables, JTAG reset (TRST), and reconfiguration

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Load TAP_3DCR (IR=0x0E) via `OcahJtagTap`.
. Configure the SEP STAP: open the SEP SIB, write SEP 3DCR `stap_sel=1, tms_hold=1, config_hold=1`, then close the SEP SIB in the same DR scan; DS and extra STAPs stay bypassed.
. Scan the extended IR: PTAP BYPASS, STAP SIB bits kept closed, SEP PTAP instruction `opcode=0x3A` through the chain.
. For each of the 6 data patterns, scan the extended DR through PTAP BYPASS {plus} STAP SIB bits {plus} SEP downstream TAP DR; read and verify the SEP PTAP model.
. Security gating:
a. Drive `feat_ctrl_i.sep_debug=0`, reconfigure, and verify the SEP PTAP model is unchanged (gated by `!sep_debug || !soc_debug || !ap_debug`).
b. Restore required enables, assert JTAG reset (TRST) to clear the stale `stap_sel`.
c. Reconfigure, re-scan extended IR/DR, and verify the SEP PTAP model updates (access restored).

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`stap_sep_select_chk` |SEP STAP TAP control active and SEP SIB closed after configuration
|`stap_sep_data_chk` |All 6 data patterns match the SEP PTAP slave model readback
|`stap_sep_gate_chk` |SEP PTAP model unchanged while `sep_debug`=1
|`stap_sep_recover_chk` |SEP access restored after restoring enables {plus} TRST {plus} reconfiguration
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The SEP STAP is selected with the SIB closed, the opcode-0x3A IR and all six patterns verify, and `!sep_debug || !soc_debug || !ap_debug` gating plus TRST-based recovery behave correctly, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-510-dtp_ext_stap_scan_test]]
===== 5.10 `dtp_ext_stap_scan_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_ext_stap_scan_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, OCAH-local STAP model

*Objective*: Extended STAP scan

*Test Coverage*:

* Extended STAP scan interface (`+jtag_stap_host_scan_*+`) enabled via PTAP_3DCR.SELECT=1
* `jtag_stap_host_scan_ctrl_o` asserts scan control during DR operations and data routes through `jtag_stap_host_scan_out_o` / `jtag_stap_host_scan_in_i`
* Clearing PTAP_3DCR.SELECT=0 deasserts the scan interface
* `stap_host_security_disable = !ap_debug` forces `jtag_stap_host_scan_ctrl_o` to 0 and bypasses scan-in (PTAP receives the `stap_host_scan_out` loopback instead of external scan-in)
* Normal scan operation resumes after `ap_debug=1`

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Enable the extended STAP scan interface by writing PTAP_3DCR.SELECT=1 via `OcahJtagTap`.
. Verify `jtag_stap_host_scan_ctrl_o` asserts during DR operations; shift a known pattern out on `jtag_stap_host_scan_out_o`.
. Verify the data returned on `jtag_stap_host_scan_in_i` matches the expected loopback/known response.
. Clear PTAP_3DCR.SELECT=0 and verify scan control deasserts.
. Security gating:
a. Re-enable scan (PTAP_3DCR.SELECT=1), then drive `feat_ctrl_i.ap_debug=0`.
b. Verify `jtag_stap_host_scan_ctrl_o` is forced to 0 and scan-in is disconnected (PTAP sees the `stap_host_scan_out` loopback rather than external scan-in).
c. Restore `ap_debug=1` and verify scan control and the data path are restored.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`ext_stap_enable_chk` |Scan control and data signals function when PTAP_3DCR.SELECT=1
|`ext_stap_disable_chk` |Scan interface deactivates when PTAP_3DCR.SELECT=0
|`ext_stap_gate_chk` |`ap_debug`=1 forces `stap_host_scan_ctrl_o` to 0 and bypasses scan-in
|`ext_stap_restore_chk` |Normal STAP host scan resumes after `ap_debug` clears
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The extended STAP scan interface operates when selected, deactivates when deselected, is suppressed and loopback-bypassed when `ap_debug=0`, and recovers when restored, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-511-dtp_3dcr_config_hold_test]]
===== 5.11 `dtp_3dcr_config_hold_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_3dcr_config_hold_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, OCAH-local STAP model

*Objective*: 3DCR config hold

*Test Coverage*:

* CONFIG_HOLD in both PTAP_3DCR and STAP 3DCR protects register contents across Test-Logic-Reset (TLR)
* With CONFIG_HOLD=1, SELECT (PTAP) and SELECT/TMS_HOLD (STAP) survive a forced TLR
* With CONFIG_HOLD=0, a TLR clears the affected 3DCR fields to 0
* Configuration values are exercised through `OcahJtagTap` IR/DR scans against the STAP reference model

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Write PTAP_3DCR with CONFIG_HOLD=1, SELECT=1; Update-DR.
. Write the STAP 3DCR with CONFIG_HOLD=1, SELECT=1, TMS_HOLD=1; Update-DR.
. Force TLR (5x TMS=1).
a. Read PTAP_3DCR and verify SELECT=1 preserved.
b. Read the STAP 3DCR and verify SELECT=1 and TMS_HOLD=1 preserved.
. Clear CONFIG_HOLD=0 in both PTAP and STAP 3DCRs; force TLR again.
a. Read both 3DCRs and verify all bits cleared to 0.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`config_hold_preserve_chk` |CONFIG_HOLD=1 preserves PTAP SELECT and STAP SELECT/TMS_HOLD across TLR
|`config_hold_clear_chk` |CONFIG_HOLD=0 lets TLR clear the 3DCR fields to 0
|`config_hold_data_chk` |3DCR readback through `OcahJtagTap` matches the STAP reference model
|===

*Pass Criteria*: CONFIG_HOLD=1 holds both 3DCRs across TLR while CONFIG_HOLD=0 allows TLR to clear them, matching the reference model, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-512-dtp_3dcr_tms_hold_test]]
===== 5.12 `dtp_3dcr_tms_hold_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_3dcr_tms_hold_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`, OCAH-local STAP model

*Objective*: 3DCR TMS hold

*Test Coverage*:

* TMS_HOLD field in the STAP 3DCR sets the parked TMS value driven to a deselected STAP
* TMS_HOLD=1 parks the deselected STAP TMS high (drives toward TLR); TMS_HOLD=0 parks it low (drives toward RTI)
* Parked value is observed on the STAP TAP control output while the STAP is deselected (SELECT=0)
* Both TMS_HOLD polarities are exercised through `OcahJtagTap` 3DCR scans

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Select a STAP via the 3DCR (SELECT=1, TMS_HOLD=1); Update-DR.
. Deselect the STAP (SELECT=0, TMS_HOLD=1); Update-DR.
a. Verify the STAP TAP control output drives TMS=1 (parked high toward TLR).
. Repeat with TMS_HOLD=0:
a. Select then deselect the STAP with TMS_HOLD=0; Update-DR.
b. Verify the STAP TAP control output drives TMS=0 (parked low toward RTI).
. Confirm the parked TMS tracks the configured TMS_HOLD against the STAP reference model.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`tms_hold_high_chk` |Deselected STAP TMS parks high (TLR) when TMS_HOLD=1
|`tms_hold_low_chk` |Deselected STAP TMS parks low (RTI) when TMS_HOLD=0
|`tms_hold_data_chk` |Parked TMS value matches the STAP reference model for each polarity
|===

*Pass Criteria*: A deselected STAP parks its TMS at the configured TMS_HOLD value for both polarities, matching the reference model, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-6-xtrig-ctp-and-ctm-scenarios]]
==== 6. XTRIG, CTP, and CTM Scenarios

[[sec-61-dtp_xtrig_wire_or_test]]
===== 6.1 `dtp_xtrig_wire_or_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_xtrig_wire_or_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local CTP/CTM BFMs

*Objective*: CTP wire-OR

*Test Coverage*:

* CTP wire-OR mode (CONFIG.MODE=0) pulse synchronization with configurable pulse stretching
* Output pulse width equals (STRETCH_MULT{plus}1) clock cycles for both a wide multiplier and the single-cycle minimum
* External CT_Req_in input synchronized into the `clk_i` domain before delivery
* STATUS.BUSY accurately reflects pulse activity (asserts during the stretched pulse, clears after)
* Internal cross-trigger pulse delivered to the CTM destination on an external trigger

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Configure CTP[0] via `OcahAxiLiteMaster`: CONFIG.MODE=0 (Wire-OR), CONFIG.INVERT=0, STRETCH_MULT=0x000F (16 clocks).
. Drive a single-cycle internal cross-trigger request to CTP[0] (via CTM routing).
a. Observe CT_Req_out on the CTP BFM; verify it asserts and remains asserted for exactly (STRETCH_MULT{plus}1) clock cycles.
b. Read CTP[0] STATUS; verify BUSY=1 while the stretched pulse is active and BUSY=0 after it completes.
. Drive a short CT_Req_in pulse from the CTP BFM (simulate an external trigger).
a. Verify the input is synchronized to `clk_i` and the resulting internal cross-trigger pulse is delivered to the programmed CTM destination.
. Reprogram STRETCH_MULT=0x0000 (1 clock), repeat the internal trigger, and verify a single-cycle output pulse with the same BUSY behavior.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`ctp_wire_or_csr_chk` |CONFIG/STRETCH_MULT writes read back the programmed route configuration
|`ctp_wire_or_stretch_chk` |Output pulse width equals (STRETCH_MULT{plus}1) clocks for both multiplier values
|`ctp_wire_or_sync_chk` |External CT_Req_in is synchronized to `clk_i` and delivered to the CTM destination
|`ctp_wire_or_busy_chk` |STATUS.BUSY asserts during the stretched pulse and clears afterward
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Wire-OR pulse stretching, input synchronization, and BUSY tracking match the reference model for both stretch settings, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-62-dtp_xtrig_p2p_test]]
===== 6.2 `dtp_xtrig_p2p_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_xtrig_p2p_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local CTP/CTM BFMs

*Objective*: CTP point-to-point

*Test Coverage*:

* CTP point-to-point 4-phase handshake (CONFIG.MODE=1) in both directions
* Request path FSM sequences REQ_PHASE1-++>++REQ_PHASE2-++>++IDLE; response path sequences RESP_PHASE1-++>++RESP_PHASE2-++>++IDLE
* STATUS register reflects live handshake state and STATUS.BUSY tracks the in-flight handshake
* Internal cross-trigger delivered to the CTM destination on an external trigger

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Configure CTP[0] via `OcahAxiLiteMaster`: CONFIG.MODE=1 (Point-to-Point), CONFIG.INVERT=0.
. Drive an internal cross-trigger request to CTP[0] (via CTM routing).
a. Verify CT_Req_out asserts on the CTP BFM.
b. Drive CT_Ack_in from the CTP BFM (simulate the external responder).
c. Verify the handshake completes: CT_Req_out deasserts, CT_Ack_in deasserts, FSM returns to IDLE.
. Read CTP[0] STATUS; verify BUSY=0 after completion.
. Drive CT_Req_in from the CTP BFM (simulate an external trigger).
a. Verify CT_Ack_out asserts and the response-side handshake completes through RESP_PHASE1-++>++RESP_PHASE2-++>++IDLE.
b. Verify the internal cross-trigger is delivered to the programmed CTM destination.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`ctp_p2p_handshake_chk` |Req/Ack 4-phase handshake completes in both directions
|`ctp_p2p_fsm_chk` |FSM sequences through REQ_PHASE1/2 and RESP_PHASE1/2 back to IDLE
|`ctp_p2p_busy_chk` |STATUS.BUSY asserts during and clears after each handshake
|`ctp_p2p_deliver_chk` |External trigger is delivered to the CTM destination
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Both handshake directions complete with correct FSM sequencing, BUSY behavior, and trigger delivery, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-63-dtp_xtrig_reset_test]]
===== 6.3 `dtp_xtrig_reset_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_xtrig_reset_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local CTP/CTM BFMs

*Objective*: CTP reset recovery

*Test Coverage*:

* CONFIG.RESET recovery from a P2P handshake deadlock (stalled external ack)
* System reset (`rst_n_i`) clears all CTP register state back to defaults
* No spurious GPIO/trigger activity asserted during either reset path
* Normal operation resumes after recovery (a fresh handshake proceeds)

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Configure CTP[0] in P2P mode and initiate a handshake.
a. Stall the external CT_Ack_in on the CTP BFM to simulate a deadlock.
b. Read STATUS; verify BUSY=1 (handshake stuck).
. Recover via CONFIG.RESET.
a. Write CONFIG.RESET=1 via `OcahAxiLiteMaster`.
b. Verify the handshake is force-cleared: STATUS.BUSY=0 and CT_Req_out deasserts.
c. Write CONFIG.RESET=0 to release; verify CTP returns to normal operation and a new handshake can proceed to completion.
. Assert system reset (`rst_n_i`).
a. Verify all CTP registers return to reset/default values on readback after release.
b. Verify no spurious GPIO toggles occur during the reset window.
. Reprogram a post-reset route and verify it operates correctly.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`ctp_config_reset_chk` |CONFIG.RESET clears a stalled P2P handshake (BUSY=0, CT_Req_out deasserts)
|`ctp_sys_reset_chk` |System reset returns all CTP CSRs to default values
|`ctp_reset_no_spurious_chk` |No spurious GPIO/trigger activity during either reset
|`ctp_reset_recovery_chk` |CTP resumes normal operation and a post-reset handshake completes
|===

*Pass Criteria*: CONFIG.RESET recovers from deadlock, system reset restores defaults with no spurious activity, and post-reset operation matches the reference model, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-64-dtp_xtrig_rand_test]]
===== 6.4 `dtp_xtrig_rand_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_xtrig_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local CTP/CTM BFMs

*Objective*: Random CTP configuration

*Test Coverage*:

* Randomized CTP configuration across multiple CTP indices, both modes, and varied settings
* MODE x INVERT x STRETCH_MULT combination coverage
* INVERT correctly flips GPIO signal polarity in both Wire-OR and P2P modes
* STRETCH_MULT respected in Wire-OR mode for each randomized value

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. For N iterations (e.g., 20):
a. Randomly select a CTP index.
b. Randomly choose a legal configuration: MODE (0/1), INVERT (0/1), STRETCH_MULT (random 16-bit).
c. Write CONFIG and STRETCH_MULT via `OcahAxiLiteMaster`.
d. Drive a cross-trigger event (internal via CTM routing or external via the CTP BFM).
e. Verify behavior matches the reference model for the configured mode and settings.
f. Verify INVERT correctly inverts the GPIO signal polarity.
. Compare the observed trigger routing/handshake stream against the software reference model across all iterations.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`ctp_rand_csr_chk` |Random CONFIG/STRETCH_MULT writes read back as programmed
|`ctp_rand_mode_chk` |Observed behavior matches the configured Wire-OR or P2P mode
|`ctp_rand_invert_chk` |INVERT flips GPIO polarity in both modes
|`ctp_rand_route_chk` |Observed trigger routing matches the reference model across iterations
|===

*Pass Criteria*: All randomized configurations produce reference-model-matching behavior, including INVERT polarity and STRETCH_MULT timing, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-65-dtp_xtrig_rand_deterministic_csr_sweep_test]]
===== 6.5 `dtp_xtrig_rand_deterministic_csr_sweep_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_xtrig_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local CTP BFM

*Objective*: CTP CSR deterministic sweep

*Test Coverage*:

* Deterministic walk of every XTRIG/CTP regblock CSR field (coverage-closure of byte-enable and masked-write condition holes)
* Per-byte byte-enable writes that random selection can miss in the CTP generated regblock
* Reserved-bit / legal-mask behavior and aliasing checks across CTP CSRs
* Generated routing state produced by each programmed value

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring the XTRIG CSR path to idle after reset and disable all CTP routing.
. Walk every CTP CSR field with deterministic values.
a. Drive full-word and per-byte byte-enable writes through `OcahAxiLiteMaster` to exercise the regblock byte-enable / masked-write conditions.
b. Cover single-bit, all-ones, and reserved-bit patterns per field.
. Read back each write and verify the defined field reflects the programmed value and that reserved bits behave as specified.
. Verify no CTP CSR aliases another (each offset is independent) and that the resulting routing state matches the reference model.
. Disable all CTP routing before test exit.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`ctp_csr_sweep_byteen_chk` |Per-byte byte-enable writes update only the addressed bytes
|`ctp_csr_sweep_readback_chk` |Every programmed CSR field reads back its written value
|`ctp_csr_sweep_reserved_chk` |Reserved bits and legal masks behave as specified
|`ctp_csr_sweep_alias_chk` |No CTP CSR offset aliases another
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every CTP CSR field accepts and returns deterministic values across all byte-enable patterns with correct reserved-bit and routing behavior and no aliasing, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-66-dtp_xtrig_rand_deterministic_dst_port_sweep_test]]
===== 6.6 `dtp_xtrig_rand_deterministic_dst_port_sweep_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_xtrig_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local CTP/CTM BFMs

*Objective*: Destination-port toggle sweep

*Test Coverage*:

* Deterministic sweep of every CTP destination-port selection (coverage-closure of destination-port toggles random selection can miss)
* Internal CT ack toggles exercised per destination
* Exactly the programmed destination toggles for a single source trigger (one-hot delivery)
* No unintended destination ports toggle

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Disable all CTM/CTP routing and idle the CSR path after reset.
. Sweep each destination port selection deterministically.
a. Program one destination-port mask at a time via `OcahAxiLiteMaster`.
b. Drive a single source trigger via CTM routing.
c. Observe the CTP/CTM BFM outputs and verify exactly the programmed destination toggles while all others remain idle.
d. Exercise the internal CT ack toggle for that destination.
. Repeat across all configured destination-port selections.
. Compare the full toggle sequence against the reference model and disable routing on exit.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`ctp_dst_port_sweep_csr_chk` |Destination-port masks read back as programmed
|`ctp_dst_port_sweep_onehot_chk` |Exactly the programmed destination toggles per source trigger
|`ctp_dst_port_sweep_ack_chk` |Internal CT ack toggles for the selected destination
|`ctp_dst_port_sweep_iso_chk` |No unprogrammed destination port toggles
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every destination-port selection toggles exactly its programmed destination with correct ack behavior and no spurious toggles, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-67-dtp_xtrig_reg_stall_test]]
===== 6.7 `dtp_xtrig_reg_stall_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_xtrig_reg_stall_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local XTRIG monitor

*Objective*: Regblock stall documentation

*Test Coverage*:

* Targets the XTRIG regblock local stall paths, expected to be structurally unreachable in this integration; intended to support a documented waiver rather than additional stimulus
* Confirms XTRIG CSR writes and reads complete without corruption when the register interface is exercised
* No trigger state changes occur until an access is accepted
* Records the integration rationale so any residual stall coverage holes can be resolved by waiver review rather than additional cocotb stimulus

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Record, in the test header, the integration rationale that the XTRIG generated-regblock local stall (back-pressure) paths are expected to be structurally unreachable in this DTP integration because the upstream AXI-Lite path always presents the regblock with a ready interface; if confirmed, they are resolved by a documented waiver rather than additional stimulus.
. Exercise representative XTRIG CSR writes and reads through `OcahAxiLiteMaster` to confirm the reachable accepted-access path.
a. Verify each write/read completes without corruption.
b. Verify no trigger state changes until the access is accepted by the regblock.
. Capture the XTRIG monitor trace as supporting evidence that the local stall condition is not asserted under the exercised stimulus, to back the waiver entry.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`xtrig_reg_stall_unreachable_chk` |Records the regblock local stall path as expected-unreachable to support a waiver entry
|`xtrig_reg_stall_csr_chk` |Accepted XTRIG CSR writes/reads read back without corruption
|`xtrig_reg_stall_no_premature_chk` |No trigger state changes until the access is accepted
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Accepted XTRIG CSR accesses complete uncorrupted, and the (expected structurally-unreachable) stall path is documented to support a waiver, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-68-dtp_xtrig_axi_channel_skew_test]]
===== 6.8 `dtp_xtrig_axi_channel_skew_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_xtrig_axi_channel_skew_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local XTRIG monitor

*Objective*: Manual AXI-Lite channel skew

*Test Coverage*:

* Manual XTRIG AXI-Lite AW/W/R/B channel skew for ready-low and response-hold coverage
* Write-channel skew: AW and W arrival offset in both orders with master-driven valid/ready timing
* Read-channel skew: AR issued with the slave R response held to exercise response-hold paths
* Protocol completion, response ordering, and final register contents preserved under skew

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Configure the XTRIG AXI-Lite master in manual mode so AW/W/AR/B/R channel timing is driven explicitly.
. Apply write-channel skew.
a. Drive AW before W (and W before AW) with a configurable gap; hold the opposite channel valid-low to create ready-low windows.
b. Verify the write completes with a correct B response and the target CSR holds the written value.
. Apply read-channel skew.
a. Issue AR, then hold the R response by keeping the master RREADY low for several cycles (response-hold).
b. Verify ARVALID/ARADDR remain stable while ARREADY is low and the read returns correct data when released.
. Verify response ordering across the skewed accesses and that final XTRIG register contents match the reference model.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`xtrig_axi_skew_aw_w_chk` |AW/W skew in both orders completes with correct B response
|`xtrig_axi_skew_ready_low_chk` |Channels remain stable through ready-low / response-hold windows
|`xtrig_axi_skew_order_chk` |Response ordering is preserved across skewed accesses
|`xtrig_axi_skew_csr_chk` |Final XTRIG CSR contents match the programmed values
|===

*Pass Criteria*: All skewed AXI-Lite write and read accesses complete with correct responses, stable channels, preserved ordering, and matching CSR contents, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-69-dtp_xtrig_axi_channel_skew_demux_aw_lock_release_test]]
===== 6.9 `dtp_xtrig_axi_channel_skew_demux_aw_lock_release_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_xtrig_axi_channel_skew_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local XTRIG monitor

*Objective*: Demux AW-lock release

*Test Coverage*:

* Focused coverage of the XTRIG `axi_lite_demux` AW-lock release branch
* AW/W arrival skew that holds the demux AW lock, then releases channels in varied order
* Lock-release branch exercised after the locked write drains
* Response ordering and final CSR contents preserved across the locked/released sequence

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Configure the XTRIG AXI-Lite master in manual mode.
. Skew AW/W arrival to hold the demux AW lock.
a. Present AW (selecting a demux slave) while delaying W so the `axi_lite_demux` AW lock is held open.
b. Confirm via the XTRIG monitor that the AW lock is engaged.
. Release the channels in varied order (W-then-B, and across back-to-back writes) to exercise the AW-lock release branch.
a. Verify the lock releases once the locked write completes and the demux is free for the next AW.
. Verify response ordering and that final XTRIG CSR contents match the reference model.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`xtrig_demux_aw_lock_engage_chk` |AW/W skew engages the `axi_lite_demux` AW lock
|`xtrig_demux_aw_lock_release_chk` |The AW-lock release branch is exercised once the locked write completes
|`xtrig_demux_order_chk` |Response ordering is preserved across the locked/released writes
|`xtrig_demux_csr_chk` |Final XTRIG CSR contents match the programmed values
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The `axi_lite_demux` AW lock engages and releases correctly with preserved ordering and matching CSR contents, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-610-dtp_xtrig_axi_channel_skew_read_decode_backpressure_test]]
===== 6.10 `dtp_xtrig_axi_channel_skew_read_decode_backpressure_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_xtrig_axi_channel_skew_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local XTRIG monitor

*Objective*: Read decode backpressure

*Test Coverage*:

* Unmapped XTRIG reads routed to the xbar default DECERR slave (`+axi_lite_xbar.default_ar_mst_port[_en]+`)
* RREADY held low so a second ARVALID must stay stable while ARREADY is low (`axi_lite_demux.ar_valid_stable` / `ar_stable`)
* Both unmapped reads return DECERR; no deadlock on the backpressured read path
* Stable ARADDR and read data through the decode-backpressure window

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Idle all manual AXI-Lite master outputs after reset; confirm the XTRIG xbar address map has no valid slave at `0x02B0` and above.
. Issue an unmapped read to `0x02B0` and hold the DECERR response by keeping the master RREADY=0.
. While the first response is held, issue a second unmapped read to `0x02F0`.
a. Verify ARVALID remains asserted and ARADDR remains stable while ARREADY is low (read-stability assertions hit).
. Release RREADY, allow the second AR to complete, and verify the second response is also DECERR.
. Confirm via the XTRIG monitor that both reads returned DECERR with no deadlock and stable channel behavior throughout.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`xtrig_read_decerr_chk` |Both unmapped reads return DECERR from the xbar default slave
|`xtrig_read_ar_stable_chk` |ARVALID/ARADDR remain stable while ARREADY is low
|`xtrig_read_default_ar_chk` |The xbar `+default_ar_mst_port[_en]+` read path receives real success hits
|`xtrig_read_no_deadlock_chk` |The backpressured read path completes without deadlock
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Both unmapped reads return DECERR with stable channels through the backpressure window and the xbar default-read and demux read-stability assertions covered, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-611-dtp_ctm_wire_or_cla_to_ctp_test]]
===== 6.11 `dtp_ctm_wire_or_cla_to_ctp_test`

*Source Module*: `dtp_ctm_wire_or_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, OCAH-local CTM/CTP BFMs

*Objective*: Wire-OR CLA to CTP

*Test Coverage*:

* CTM wire-OR (multicast) routing of one internal CLA source to multiple external CTP destinations
* `+CT_SRC[src]_CONFIG_0.CT_DST_SELECT+` programmed with several destination bits set simultaneously
* All selected CTP destinations receive the trigger on the same source pulse; non-selected ports stay idle
* Wire-OR superposition when a second overlapping CLA source targets one of the same CTP destinations
* CSR readback of the multicast destination mask matches the programmed value

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Program the multicast route via `OcahAxiLiteMaster`:
a. Write `+CT_SRC[src]_CONFIG_0.CT_DST_SELECT+` with multiple CTP destination bits set (e.g. bits 0, 5, 16).
b. Read the register back and confirm the multicast mask matches the written value.
. Drive a cross-trigger request on the CLA source via the OCAH-local CTM BFM.
. Observe the CTP destinations with the OCAH-local CTP monitor:
a. Verify every selected CTP destination asserts the trigger simultaneously.
b. Verify every non-selected destination port remains quiet.
. Exercise wire-OR superposition:
a. Program a second CLA source whose `CT_DST_SELECT` overlaps one of the same CTP destinations.
b. Pulse both sources and verify the overlapped destination ORs the contributions while disjoint destinations track only their own source.

*Checkers*:

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Checker Type |Description
|`ctm_wire_or_cla_to_ctp_csr_chk` |CSR readback of the multicast `CT_DST_SELECT` mask matches the programmed value
|`ctm_wire_or_cla_to_ctp_route_chk` |All selected CTP destinations assert on the CLA pulse against the reference model
|`ctm_wire_or_cla_to_ctp_or_chk` |Overlapping CLA sources OR together at the shared CTP destination
|`ctm_wire_or_cla_to_ctp_nospur_chk` |No non-selected destination port toggles
|===

*Pass Criteria*: The CLA source multicasts to exactly its selected CTP destinations, overlapping sources OR correctly, and no spurious destination toggles occur, with zero scoreboard/reference-model errors.

'''''

[[sec-612-dtp_ctm_wire_or_ctp_to_cla_test]]

[cols=",",options="header",]

|===
